Contrived and fairly EXCLUDED from the claim: Function(), eval, constructor.constructor, import.meta, process["env"]. `export * from` passing is CORRECT — no local binding exists. The file's own header comment makes non-ASCII correctness a stated goal (it is why the export is read from a UTF-8 file at all), so this wants fixing: measure column offsets by display width. Emoji and combining marks are NOT affected (astral pairs parse correctly - verified). t480 has no such app today, which is why 225/225 rows parse. (N2) counts.listed_apps says 1,072 while the apps table holds 1,056 and NOTHING reports the gap. S0 increments seen.listed_apps on an INSERT ... ON CONFLICT upsert, so a PK collision counts as listed. The 16 rows behind it are winget's own identical triples and carry no lost fact (see above), but a reader of the map's own count is told 1,072 rows exist. The defect is in estate_map.mjs, outside S2's allowlist, and S2 is simply the first task to exercise the apps path. For S4: reconcile counts against the table, or have putApp report whether it inserted. (N3) The winget parser will accept ANY short non-blank line after the header as an application. Feeding it a header, a good row and a 18-character continuation line yields a phantom app named 'winget' with an empty id and version. Additive, never subtractive, and it cannot happen through this code path: Out-File does not wrap (probed at 400 characters) and the real 228-line export has no line shorter than 63 characters other than the trailing blank. Worth an anchor on the row shape if winget ever gains a trailing notice. (N4) The PowerShell scripts are the largest integrity surface in the file and NOTHING tests them - six of my mutations that delete a registry hive, zero the errors counter, filter services to Running, truncate the port list or narrow winget to one source all leave the gate GREEN. Every one is a way for the map to be silently short, and the only thing standing between the estate and that today is that the shipped strings are correct - which I verified live (hives 23+28+14=65 = the collector's 65; an unreadable key returns errors:1). A cheap guard would be a shape assertion over APP_SOURCES[*].script. Two cosmetic notes, no action needed: endpoint() double-brackets an already-bracketed address ([[::1]]), which Get-NetTCPConnection never produces; and a '^'-anchored RegExp quarantine rule would be defeated by the service:/task:/port: name prefixes, while the string rules the owner actually uses are substring matches and are unaffected (proved by the privacy sweep above). The registry collector is the one source with no id-based disambiguation, so two Uninstall entries sharing a DisplayName AND DisplayVersion across HKLM/WOW6432Node/HKCU would silently fold - on t480 all 65 registry rows are distinct (65 collected, 65 distinct, 65 stored) so nothing is lost today, but the class the author closed for winget and appx is left open for registry; PSPath is the obvious identity. Scripts: scratchpad v2_mut, v2_real, v2_winget, v2_attack, v2_cjk, v2_privacy, v2_hives, v2_scen, v2_board. 74 stale %TEMP%/estate-apps-* directories left by mutation runs (mine and the author's, which include a deliberate no-cleanup mutant) were removed; the shipped gate leaks none.

WHAT THE GAP ACTUALLY IS, checked in the code rather than taken from the board note: the CONSUMER already exists and is already honest - derive_metrics in runtime/benny/sdlc/bench_executor.py computes tool_selection_accuracy from node_progress events carrying detail.tool judged against a frozen rubric's expected_ops, and tool_efficiency from min_steps. events.py already defines the node_progress emitter. What is missing is that orchestrator.py never calls it, no manifest requires a real choice of tool, and no rubric exists - so the field is null in docs/bench/results/p4-report.json and in both rung reports, with unavailable_reason pointing at the P4 amendment. P7 therefore supplies three things and changes no consumer: an agentic manifest, the orchestrator emission that records the tool chosen, and a rubric frozen before any result is seen. THE TRAP THE CONTRACT IS WRITTEN AROUND: an instrument that cannot be failed measures nothing. If every judged node admits exactly one plausible tool then tool_selection_accuracy is 1.0 by construction, which is WORSE than the unavailable it replaces because it looks like evidence. So the contract requires the manifest to offer a real choice, makes 'a deliberately wrong subject scores below 1.0' a normative Scenario, and makes a single-candidate manifest a gate FAILURE. It also holds P6's rules: a rubric matching no emitted node leaves the metric unmeasured (not 1.0 and not 0.0), and a node that emitted no tool is neither a hit nor silently dropped from the denominator. OUT OF SCOPE and stated as such: changing derive_metrics (P6 owns it and it is already correct - a defect found there is logged, not fixed); token fields on the stream; GRPO (R15); re-running P4's report with the new number. NOTE: P7's allowlist includes runtime/benny/pypes/orchestrator.py, which is also in G0's allowlist (G0 is DONE). Flagging it here so a verifier judges the overlap deliberately rather than discovering it. Not claimed - P7 sits in READY for whoever takes it.

2026-09-21T00:00:00Z | S5 S6 S7 | amended | claude-opus-5 | Owner directive 2026-09-21: 'consolidate all my backups from various drives into this main one, but it should be safe and deterministic where duplicates are identified and workflows to manage are easy.' Asked the owner two questions that change what gets built. SEQUENCING: owner chose KEEP THE PLANNED ORDER (S3 -> S4 -> S5 -> S6 -> S7) over bringing a local-only consolidation forward on S1's already-merged map, so only one integrity engine is ever written and S3 stays next. DISPOSITION: owner chose REPORT ONLY, I DECIDE EACH - a confirmed duplicate becomes a proposal with bytes, counts and sole-copy warnings, and nothing moves or is deleted unsigned. Both already matched the contracts' design (S6 raises and never executes; S7 verifies the destination before evicting). WHAT THE CONTRACTS DID NOT CARRY, now amended into all three (commit d65bc55e): THE DRIVES COME AND GO. 'Various drives' are externals attached intermittently, so an unattached or stale device is not evidence either way. S5: coverage names the devices it counted and when each was last swept; a device whose sweep errored or predates the content's mtime is reported stale, counted as 0, and can never make something 'not a sole copy'; classification is a function of content and device identity, never of sweep order (two new Scenarios). S6: backup sets are declared TREE ROOTS being merged into one home, not a category - a proposal is about CONTENT, naming the hash, every path holding it, the surviving path and the declared deterministic rule that chose it; suspected (partial-hash) content and content whose other copies sit on a stale device may be proposed for COPYING but never for REMOVAL (three new Scenarios). S7: a removal needs a survivor verified by FULL HASH on an ATTACHED device immediately beforehand; an unplugged survivor aborts the eviction, leaves every copy in place and leaves the proposal runnable; eviction is per content, each preceded by its own verification, never a batch shortcut (two new Scenarios). GROUND TRUTH gathered before amending: C: 475.9 GB with 47.6 free (nearly full), F: 'Elements' 4657.5 GB with 3207.7 free - F: is the target and is already the accumulation problem, holding machine-shaped trees from earlier consolidations (Dell Inspiron 6400, lenovo, Optimus, backups, backup-preuninstall-20260904-224648, _Serato_Backup, phone) alongside nas, media, benny-home and estate-store. No N: mapped at present. Nothing claimed or started: S3 is next on the plan and is human-signed, so it waits on the owner.

Branch task/S12 @ 9da4f311. Gate node scripts/gates/s2.mjs GREEN 35/35 (33 before); s0 42/42 and s1 21/21 still green. Budget 56/150 non-test (49 added / 7 removed, lib only; the gate script is untouched). WHY THIS EXISTS: claude-s11-verifier PASSED S11 and then showed that S11 REGRESSED A NARROWER CASE THAN THE ONE IT CLOSED. I hand-wrote the wide-character ranges; U+1F642 has been East Asian Width Wide since Unicode 9 and winget pads it as two columns, so an emoji in an application name threw WINGET_MALFORMED on S11 where it parsed on MAIN. Same for variation selectors, ZWJ, skin-tone modifiers and combining marks, which S11 counted as ZERO columns and winget counts as one. Loud, not silent, but the exact shape S11 existed to close. AND WHY MY TESTS DID NOT CATCH IT (the verifier's N2, which is the real defect): the emoji fixture was padded with the library's own displayWidth and then displayWidth was asserted against it - self-consistent and blind by construction - while the assertion displayWidth(emoji) === 1 was labelled 'the widths winget actually uses'. It asserted a falsehood as fact and would have agreed with ANY table. THE CLASS, NOT THE INSTANCE: a hand-kept range list drifts from the standard silently and is only ever corrected one block at a time. The table is now GENERATED from the Unicode Character Database's own EastAsianWidth.txt (17.0.0, matching this Node's ICU 17.0), fetched once at authoring time and encoded into the file as base-36 gap/span pairs: 293 declared ranges merged to 123, 570 characters, decoded once into a flat array and searched binary. The file records the Unicode version it came from. DERIVING IT IMMEDIATELY CAUGHT A WRONG SUGGESTION: the verifier's N3 proposed adding Hangul Jamo Extended-A AND Extended-B by eye. The standard classes Ext-A as W and Ext-B (D7B0..D7C6) as N, so hand-adding both would have shipped a NEW wrong answer. The vendored eastasianwidth package was rejected as a source for the same reason - it is Unicode 8-era and calls U+1F642 and Tangut narrow, i.e. it reproduces the very bug. THE RULE IS NOW THE WHOLE RULE: EAW W or F is two columns, everything else is one, counted per CODE POINT. S11's zero-width class is gone. That is what claude-s11-verifier measured winget doing (Han, Hangul and U+FF1A pad as 2; Cyrillic, U+00AE and U+2013 as 1). TESTS NO LONGER PAD WITH THE CODE UNDER TEST: the emoji row is padded BY HAND to the EAW rule with the arithmetic in a comment; the width cases are asserted against the standard including a range's final code point (U+A97C), plane 2 (U+20000), the last range's end (U+3FFFD) and a NARROW astral character (U+1D400, one column); and a VERBATIM capture of real `winget search --query <japanese>` output taken on this machine on 2026-09-21 is parsed whole - three real rows (a Store language pack, CeVIO AI Voice Package - one with mixed Latin/CJK and parentheses, and Google Japanese IME) with their real ids and versions. Real winget padding, not ours: if the width rule is wrong those rows do not line up. MUTATIONS (scratchpad/s12_mutations.mjs): 18/18 KILLED, lib restored byte-identical. Everything one column, everything two, S11's zero-width rule restored, astral pairs split, charCodeAt for codePointAt, the last wide ranges dropped or truncated, the gap decoding off by one, gaps read as absolute, base 10 for base 36, both binary-search boundary errors, nothing ever wide, plus the S11/S2 rules underneath. WHAT MAY CROSS IS AN ALLOWLIST, NOT A DENYLIST. The N7 session guard rejects fields named content/text; for a map that is the wrong shape, because the next field anybody invents walks through. mapPayloadProblem() names every field that may cross - counts, statuses, category labels, volume names, hashes - and refuses the payload whole if it sees anything else. A refused push records NOTHING, not even last-seen: the whole push is refused, not the bad part of it. VOLUME NAMES are the one path-shaped string allowed through (the hub cannot report where a sweep ran without them), so they are bounded at 260 characters and judged against the HUB's quarantine rules, not the node's - a node that forgot a rule, or was asked to sweep a volume the hub considers private, cannot put that name in the estate map. Collector and total volumes are judged the same way. A rejection names the FIELD and never the value, so the refusal is not itself a side channel. THE FOLD: latest sweep per machine, latest listing per volume. An older sweep never overwrites a newer one, but it MAY still fill a volume the newer sweep did not cover - a drive that was not swept this time is not a drive that vanished, and it carries the time it was last seen. Equal finished_at breaks by sweep id, so network ordering cannot change the map (asserted by folding the same two pushes in both orders and deepEqual-ing the result). A node that stops pushing is stale SINCE a time with its volumes and counts intact (plan SS7: no false outages), and a machine the hub has never heard from is 404, not an empty machine. SO THE QUESTION IS INVERTED AND THE CHECK IS A CHOKE POINT. The panel's OWN stylesheet is read as source text and refused if it carries anything that could remove content: ANY at-rule at all, and a list of VALUES rather than a list of tricks (display:none, visibility other than visible, opacity under .5, any transform/clip/clip-path/filter/content-visibility/pointer-events, zero height/width/max-*, unreadable font sizes, the font shorthand, negative letter-spacing or text-indent, transparent text, fixed/sticky positions, offsets past the viewport). The panel is ours, it is 410 lines, and it has no legitimate need to hide anything - the states that come and go are x-if, not CSS - so the honest answer is 'none of that appears here' rather than 'jsdom could not see it'. E4 CLOSED BY DELETION: the flattener counted braces without knowing about strings, so one content:'{' left it returning the stylesheet unflattened - failing OPEN, silently, for the whole file. There is nothing to flatten now; an at-rule is simply refused. E5 CLOSED, AND IT IS THE ONE I SHOULD HAVE SEEN MYSELF: blinding hiddenBy(), blinding the stylesheet check, removing the stylesheet from the document under test and making until() return quietly on timeout EACH SHIPPED GREEN. Every guard I had added to the panel was itself unguarded - B4's class applied to the thing built to answer B4. All four now kill the gate, and the checker is pinned by a table of the 24 attacks that have been used against this panel plus the brace-in-a-string case. E2 CLOSED: the rendered area test ran at ONE box, 640x400, which no browser produces - so a distortion conditioned on the ASPECT RATIO shipped green, and at the 932x416 a browser really gives the panel it drew one block labelled 't480 1.1 MB' (a file count formatted as bytes) while pi and optimus, the two largest nodes, vanished. Areas are now checked at five shapes including that one, at three estate sizes, AND the treemap must tile its box - which kills the half-box layout that kept every proportion intact while drawing the estate at half scale. E3 CLOSED: bytes.drawn was computed in storageView, which has never seen the box, so it counted blocks the layout marks too-small and the view refuses to render. Two machines at a 1000:1 byte ratio is enough. On a fifty-node fold that was ONE block on the map above a line claiming every byte was drawn, with 98 'too small to draw' entries printed directly beneath it.
